# SEAMS.md — Knauf Upstream Modifications

## Upstream Base

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| LibreChat tag | `v0.8.7` |
| Base tag | `knauf/base-v0.8.7` |
| Last synced | 2026-05-07 |

The `LibreChat tag` row is the upstream baseline used by [`upstream-release-watcher.yml`](.github/workflows/upstream-release-watcher.yml) to compute `upstream/<base>..upstream/<latest>` diffs. It must be a real tag in `danny-avila/LibreChat`. The `Base tag` row is the Knauf-side anchor used by humans to identify the merge commit on the fork.

## Inline Modifications

> **Append-only.** Each ticket appends its own row(s); prior rows are never edited. A single upstream file may appear in multiple rows, one per ticket that touched it. The `Ticket` column carries the originating Jira ID (`KK20-XXX`), or `—` for rows that predate the convention. `Lines` is captured at the commit that added the seam; drift after subsequent reformats is accepted. See [.claude/skills/seam-manifest/SKILL.md](.claude/skills/seam-manifest/SKILL.md).

| `packages/api/src/auth/ip.ts` | `isPrivateIP` top-of-body normalisation | 114–138 | KK20-194 — close non-canonical IPv6 bypass class (same as the one patched in commit 37136c54 for `open_url`). **Relocated from `packages/api/src/auth/domain.ts` in the v0.8.6 merge** — upstream moved `isPrivateIP` (and its IPv4/IPv6 helpers) into the new `ip.ts` and `domain.ts` now re-exports the symbol; the hardening was re-applied at its new home. Upstream's regex matchers in `isPrivateIP` only recognise canonical compressed forms (`::ffff:7f00:1`, `64:ff9b::a9fe:a9fe`); fully-expanded variants (`0:0:0:0:0:ffff:7f00:1`, zero-padded forms, expanded NAT64) walk past every matcher. The seam canonicalises any IPv6 input via the WHATWG URL parser (which compresses + strips leading zeros) BEFORE the existing matchers run. This single seam hardens both the upstream MCP HTTP transport (which calls `isPrivateIP` via `createSSRFSafeUndiciConnect`) AND Knauf's `open_url` tool (which delegates SSRF entirely to the same upstream helper). | `api/app/clients/tools/structured/specs/OpenUrl.spec.js` (upstream `isPrivateIP` seam describe block, 11 cases), `packages/api/src/auth/domain.spec.ts` (KK20-194 describe blocks) | KK20-194 |

PR #98 review (R2b): after reading the body, raw-HTML anti-bot/JS-challenge markers (Cloudflare/DataDome/PerimeterX) throw `FetchError('blocked_or_js_required')` so a challenge interstitial is never extracted as content. HTML extraction + envelope wrapping delegated to `@knauf/knauf/server/htmlExtract`. | `packages/api/src/tools/registry/definitions.ts` | `openUrlSchema` / `openUrlDescription` consts + `toolDefinitions.open_url` entry | 357–394, 491–500 | KK20-194 — register the Knauf-only `open_url` tool in the upstream tool-definitions registry. Without an entry, `getToolDefinition('open_url')` returns `undefined` and the model-facing definitions path (`loadToolDefinitions`) silently skips it, so the model never receives its schema — i.e. the tool was uncallable via agents. The consts are the single source of truth: `OpenUrl.js` pulls its `description`/`schema` from `getToolDefinition('open_url')` so picker, model schema, and instance description cannot drift. The description also encodes the routing contract: open_url is authoritative for a named/pasted page, and on an open_url error/blocked code (e.g. `blocked_or_js_required`, `http_404`, `timeout`, `ssrf_blocked`) the model falls back to Azure-native `web_search`. | `packages/api/src/tools/registry/open-url.knauf.spec.ts` | KK20-194 |

A shared `gateOpenUrlOnWebSearch` helper binds `open_url` presence to the **raw browse intent** — `web_search` present in the agent's OWN `tools` — and **strips** any pre-existing `open_url` when that intent is absent, so an agent with a baked-in `open_url` stays fully controlled by the switch. The gate keys on the raw `agent.tools.includes(web_search)`, NOT the capability-filtered list: `web_search` is deliberately kept out of `endpoints.agents.capabilities`, so the provider-less LangChain `web_search` tool is stripped/unsurfaced (no third-party search provider — GDPR), while open_url is still injected. The model therefore sees exactly `open_url` (authoritative for named/pasted pages) + Azure-native model-level `web_search` (open-ended discovery + fallback). Applied at three chokepoints: model-facing definitions path (authoritative), runtime construction path, and a fail-closed guard in `loadToolsForExecution`. | `api/server/services/__tests__/ToolService.open-url.knauf.spec.js` | KK20-194 |

Upstream enforced this permission only on the skills REST routes (`api/server/routes/skills.js`) and inside `canCreateSkill`, which gates authoring; nothing checked it while building a run. `accessibleSkillIds` came from `findAccessibleResources` (resource ACLs — a different authorization axis) and `mergeDeploymentSkillIds` then appends every deployment skill unconditionally by design, since deployment skills carry no ACL rows. Net effect: a role with `SKILLS.USE: false` still received the deployment catalog and could invoke `karl-brand` / `karl-artifacts`. Uses the request-cached checker because `canCreateSkill` reads the same role document in the same request. | `client/src/hooks/Artifacts/useArtifacts.ts` | `userSelectedTabRef` / `selectActiveTab` + end-of-turn tab reconciliation | 14–38, 148–166, 203–206, 216–219 | KK20-252 — the artifacts panel could pin itself to the Code tab and never come back, so a user who clicked an artifact card mid-stream was left looking at Monaco source where a render belonged. Upstream switches to `code` while an artifact streams, and the only route back is a regex over the raw message text gated on `isSubmitting` (`:139`) — so it cannot fire once the turn ends, and any emission it fails to match (truncated, prose between the directive line and the fence, two fences, or a `}` inside a title attribute, which defeats its `[^}]*`) pinned the tab for the rest of the turn. Upstream's own guard for entering `code` is vacuous — it tests `latestMessageText.includes(artifact.content.slice(0, 50))`, and `artifact.content` is `extractContent(props.children)`, i.e. a substring of that text by construction — so the switch fires on every artifact. Rather than rewrite the streaming heuristic (its watch-it-type behaviour is wanted), this adds an unconditional reconciliation on the streaming→idle transition, suppressed only when the user picked the tab themselves. `setActiveTab` is returned as a wrapper that records the choice; in-hook effects keep the raw setter so automatic switches stay automatic; the flag resets per message so one manual pick cannot mute reconciliation forever | `client/src/hooks/Artifacts/__tests__/useArtifacts.test.ts` (`[knauf] useArtifacts — tab reconciliation after generation`, 5 cases, 3 negative-tested) | KK20-252 |
| `client/src/hooks/Artifacts/__tests__/useArtifacts.test.ts` | `[knauf] useArtifacts — tab reconciliation after generation` | 850–972 | KK20-252 — seam test. Populates the recoil mock with a real artifact map, which the pre-existing suite in this file does not: it leaves `useRecoilValue` at `{}`, so `orderedArtifactIds.length === 0` short-circuits before any tab logic runs and every `expect(activeTab).toBe('preview')` there passes on the `useState` default no matter what the hook does. Covers the streaming→idle return, the brace-in-title shape that defeats upstream's enclosure regex on a well-formed artifact, that a deliberate tab choice is never overridden, that the choice is scoped to its own run, and that code-only artifacts are untouched. Verified: disabling the reconciliation fails exactly 3 of the 5 — the other two are non-regression assertions and correctly pass either way | Self | KK20-252 |
| `client/src/components/Artifacts/Artifact.tsx` | `extractArtifactBody` call in `updateArtifact` | 10–13, 71–79 | KK20-252 — upstream built the artifact's content with `extractContent(props.children)`, which is `children.map(extractContent).join('')`: every child of the `:::artifact` container concatenated with no separator. A well-formed artifact is exactly one fenced block, so the concatenation was a no-op and the defect stayed invisible. When the model appends anything after the fence, all of it lands in `App.tsx` and Sandpack throws `Unexpected token` — observed twice on this ticket, once on a trailing `<!-- ... shadcn/ui ... -->` comment and once on a trailing `<!-- ... -->` plus `<style>` block. Verified against this repo's own mdast/hast stack that the parser already classifies those trailing nodes as `html`/`raw` and hands them over regardless: children parse as `code(lang=tsx), html, html` → `element(pre), raw, raw`. Five prompt revisions on this ticket each relocated the behaviour rather than removing it, which is why this is a code fix; the prompt still asks for one fence, and this makes it not matter when the model does otherwise | `client/src/knauf/utils/artifactBody.knauf.spec.tsx` (8 cases, 4 negative-tested) | KK20-252 |

Removed in PR #126. **Next upstream sync:** if upstream still carries/modifies these paths, `git merge` will raise a modify/delete conflict — resolve by keeping them deleted, not restoring upstream's version. |

| `librechat.yaml` | KK20-194 — exposes the native `open_url` tool via `includedTools`. The model is told to call it (and how to interpret the `<UNTRUSTED_WEB_CONTENT>` envelope) via the Tool's own `description`, not a prompt-prefix paragraph — so the guidance follows the tool to any future agent. Also defaults the "Browse the internet" capability ON: `webSearch: true` on the `karl` + `karl-personality-*` modelSpecs (KARL has no UI toggle), the azureOpenAI group `addParams.web_search: true` (Azure-native model-level search always-on for KARL), and `web_search` deliberately kept OUT of `endpoints.agents.capabilities` (the capabilities comment block documents why — the provider-less LangChain web_search tool must stay unsurfaced; the gate is raw-intent in ToolService.js). See `docs/kk20-194-open-url-tool-proposal.md`. |
| `api/app/clients/tools/manifest.json` | KK20-194 — registers the `open_url` plugin entry (no `authConfig`, no API key). JSON has no comment syntax, so a `[knauf]` marker is not possible — tracked here instead. |
| `packages/knauf/package.json` | KK20-194 — adds server-side deps for the open_url tool (`@mozilla/readability`, `jsdom`, `html-to-text`) and an `exports` map (`./server/htmlExtract`) so `api/` can require the Knauf HTML extraction helper without bundling jsdom into the upstream lockfile. All three deps are permissive (MIT / Apache-2.0). SSRF protection itself is delegated to upstream's `createSSRFSafeUndiciConnect` (see `[knauf]` seam in `packages/api/src/auth/domain.ts`), so no IP-parser dep is needed here. |
| `api/package.json`, `packages/api/package.json` | Raises `@librechat/agents` to `^3.2.65`, adopting upstream Langfuse-team trace shaping: stable agent/tool names, scoped tool args, clean root question/answer, and dropped LangGraph plumbing spans |

The dev worker VALUES bump itself lives in PR #151. |

Pure documentation block, no active keys. See `knauf/docs/implementation-plans/code-interpreter-artifacts-rollout.md`. |

| `client/src/locales/{en,de}/translation.json` | Localizes the exact aggregate-size partial-acceptance warning and keeps the supported-upload dialog synchronized with the 5-file / 100 MB chat policy. |
| `client/src/locales/{en,de}/translation.json` | Localizes the upload-rate-limit wait message shown while the serialized client queue honors `Retry-After` and retries. |
| `.env.example` | Raises the documented per-scope async-indexing backlog default from 20 to 50 files while retaining the independent 500 MB byte ceiling. |
| `client/src/locales/{en,de}/translation.json` | Localizes agent-wide ready/processing/failed counts and distinct backlog-wait versus manual-retry guidance. |
| `librechat.yaml` | KK20-243 — activates `artifacts` + `skills` in `endpoints.agents.capabilities`. That list is the only gate for artifacts (there is no RBAC permission for it) and the first of three for skills. The block's explanatory comment is rewritten: it claimed to restrict "the agent builder", which was wrong — because `capabilities` is set explicitly, upstream's `defaultAgentCapabilities` never applies and this list gates every chat, including default-spec KARL chats on the `azureOpenAI` endpoint (all sends route through `api/server/routes/agents/chat.js`; `skipAgentCheck` exists to let non-agents endpoints through). `execute_code` stays excluded — the sandbox is not deployed. Adds `interface.skills: {use:true, create:false, share:false, public:false}` so `hasExplicitConfig` rewrites the USER/ADMIN role docs on every boot instead of leaving them frozen at first-create; all four keys are spelled out because the conditional spreads only emit SHARE/SHARE_PUBLIC when `share` or `public` is present. Sets `artifacts: 'default'` and `skills: true` on the `karl` modelSpec — `applyModelSpecEphemeralAgent` is authoritative for new spec-driven conversations and writes `artifacts: ''` when the key is absent, so without it artifacts is forced off on every new chat. AGENT_BUILDER is a separate manual step — see `knauf/scripts/seed-agent-builder-role.js`. |
| `.env.example` | KK20-243 — corrects the KK20-108 block, which asserted artifacts and skills were "kept dark on KARL"; that is now true only of `execute_code`. Rewrites the `SANDPACK_BUNDLER_URL` / `SANDPACK_STATIC_BUNDLER_URL` notes from hypothetical to operational: both are read per-environment at `api/server/routes/config.js` so they can differ across dev/stage/prod without an image rebuild, any network allow-list must use a wildcard `*.codesandbox.io` because the bundler host encodes the sandpack-client version and moves on a dependency bump, and `cdn.tailwindcss.com` is hardcoded in `client/src/utils/artifacts.ts` with no env override — if corporate egress blocks it, React previews render unstyled and the fix is a code change. |
| `.dockerignore` | KK20-243 — excludes `knauf/brand/` (~26 MB of source-of-truth EPS/PDF/font-archive design assets) from the `az acr build` context. Reference material only; nothing there is read at runtime, and the palette plus wordmark that artifacts need are transcribed as text into `skill/karl-brand/` instead. `skill/` is deliberately NOT excluded — deployment skills must reach `/app/skill` via the root Dockerfile's blanket `COPY . .`, and `.dockerignore` has no `skill` pattern. |
| `.gitattributes` | KK20-243 — marks the `knauf/brand/**` binaries (`*.eps`, `*.pdf`, `*.7z`, `*.png`) as `-text -diff` so git never EOL-normalises or attempts to diff them; a textual diff of a 12 MB PDF is noise at best. The `.md` and `.html` files in that tree stay normal text. |
| `knauf/scripts/seed-agent-builder-role.js` | KK20-243 — grants the AGENT_BUILDER role `SKILLS: {USE: true, CREATE: false, SHARE: false, SHARE_PUBLIC: false}`; it was hard-set all-false, so enabling the skills capability would still have left AI Champions with no `$` popover, no composer chip and no sidebar catalog. CREATE stays false because authoring belongs to platform engineering, matching `interface.skills`. This must stay explicit: the script does `$set: { permissions }`, a full replace, and `updateInterfacePermissions` only ever iterates USER and ADMIN, so nothing else writes SKILLS onto this role and a missing permission type reads as deny. Operationally it is neither a job nor a migration — a human runs it via mongosh per environment after each release, and running an older checkout afterwards silently reverts SKILLS to false. Also adds a `/* global db, printjson */` directive and a `prettier-ignore` over the aligned PERMISSIONS table so the file passes the pre-commit gates it had never been staged against. |
| `client/src/locales/{en,de}/translation.json` | KK20-243 — adds four `com_knauf_capability_*` keys for the two new agent-builder capability rows (artifacts, skills). JSON has no comment syntax, so no `[knauf]` marker is possible; `seams.js diff-check` also skips this file because `.json` is not a source extension, which is why this row is hand-authored. Keys are inserted in place rather than by re-sorting the file, which would move unrelated entries. The registry keeps its English literals as a documented fallback for `browseInternet`, which predates localization in that panel; `client/src/knauf/components/Agents/capabilities.knauf.spec.ts` asserts every declared key resolves in both shipped locales. |
| `librechat.yaml` | KK20-243 (follow-up to the row above, appended rather than edited per the append-only rule) — extends `artifacts: 'default'` + `skills: true` from the `karl` spec to all three `karl-personality-*` specs. Those three are selectable in the chat model dropdown, not only by the agent-builder pills: `interface.modelSelect` is true and none sets `group`, so `ModelSelector` renders them via `modelSpecs.filter((spec) => !spec.group)`. Because the spec is authoritative for a new conversation and an absent key reads as "off" rather than "inherit", switching personality silently disabled both features — `applyModelSpecEphemeralAgent` writes `artifacts: ''` and `applyModelSpecSkills` (`packages/api/src/agents/added.ts`) early-returns so `skills_enabled` never becomes true. Same class of bug KK20-194 fixed for `webSearch` on the same three specs, and the block comment now states the parity rule so a future capability added to `karl` is not forgotten here. `karl-autogenerate-prompt` is deliberately left without these keys — it is the internal ✦ drafter, not a user surface. Also corrects a stale path in that block's comment (`client/src/custom/knauf/agents/` → `client/src/knauf/data-provider/Agents/`). |
| `librechat.yaml` | KK20-252 (appended, not edited into the KK20-243 rows above, per the append-only rule) — fixes three defects KK20-243 shipped, all confirmed against a Langfuse trace. **(1)** The `&karl_prompt` `Files:` paragraph ordered the model to "explain that file generation is not supported … respond as text within the chat", which landed in the same system message as the ~2.9k-token artifacts prompt telling it artifacts are for "reports, emails, presentations". Asked for a dashboard the model refused outright. Split into an `Artifacts:` paragraph that affirmatively permits the preview panel and a narrowed `Files:` paragraph that still forbids the binary formats we genuinely cannot produce (PDF/PPTX/DOCX/XLSX/ZIP/audio/video). Applies to all four user-facing specs through the anchor. **(2)** `maxContextTokens: 128000` on every spec preset. Nothing set it, and `model-router-chat` / `agent-default` are Knauf aliases that `getModelMaxTokens` cannot resolve, so `initialize.ts:1042-1050` fell back to `DEFAULT_MAX_CONTEXT_TOKENS = 32000` — a ~28.9k pruning budget minus ~5k of instructions, and a skill catalog budgeted at 1% of 32k. 128k is a deliberate conservative floor for Model Router's candidate pool pending infra confirmation; an explicit pin also bypasses the 0.95 reserve, so the value is used verbatim. **(3)** A `summarization:` block. Its absence did not mean off: `resolveSummarizationConfig` tests `config?.enabled !== false`, so `undefined !== false` had summarization enabled by accident. Left enabled — better for recall than hard truncation — but now discoverable, with the still-defaulted provider/model/trigger/prompt called out in the comment. |
| `infrastructure/stage/values.yaml` | Temporarily renders the AGK stage app Deployment with `Recreate` so its first generation-scoped Redis release cannot mix old and new API protocols; remove after that rollout completes. |
| `infrastructure/prod/values.yaml` | Temporarily renders the AGK prod app Deployment with `Recreate` so its first generation-scoped Redis release cannot mix old and new API protocols; remove after that rollout completes. |
| `client/src/locales/{en,de}/translation.json` | Artifact-only sharing copy — localizes the direct share action, loading state, and error message (NO-TICKET) |
| `librechat.yaml` | KK20-252 (second append for this ticket, kept separate from the row above per the append-only rule) — makes artifact creation proactive. The `&karl_prompt` `Artifacts:` paragraph now tells the model to reach for an artifact on its own initiative whenever the output is a *deliverable* (dashboard, report, briefing, deck, diagram, calculator, standalone doc, runnable code or interactive UI), and adds a matching `Keeping it in the chat:` paragraph so ordinary answers, explanations, short comparisons and anything under a screenful stay inline. Written here rather than in `skill/karl-artifacts/` because a skill body only reaches the model once the skill is invoked, and the whole point is behaviour with no invocation. Also replaces the positional phrase "nothing in the next paragraph restricts it" with a named reference to the `Files:` rule — the original silently detaches if a paragraph is ever inserted between the two, with every test still green. Reaches all four user-facing specs through the anchor; `karl-autogenerate-prompt` is unaffected. |
| `librechat.yaml` | KK20-252 (third append for this ticket) — `interface.runCode: false`. Run Code is now off for every role. `execute_code` was already excluded from `endpoints.agents.capabilities` (KK20-108/KK20-243: the self-hosted sandbox is not deployed on AGK, so the tool always fails) and built-in USER already had RUN_CODE.USE forced false by `KNAUF_RESTRICT_USER_PERMISSIONS`, which left ADMIN seeded with a manual Run button backed by nothing. Removing the permission rather than hiding the chip drops both `CodeInterpreter.tsx` and the tools-menu entry, which gate on `useHasAccess(RUN_CODE, USE)`. **Must move together with `knauf/scripts/seed-agent-builder-role.js`** — `updateInterfacePermissions` (`packages/api/src/app/permissions.ts:125`) iterates only USER and ADMIN, so a custom role's grant is never revoked from this file. Pinned by `librechatYaml.knauf.spec.ts` (`Run Code stays off`, 2 cases, negative-tested). |
| `librechat.yaml` | KK20-252 (fourth append for this ticket) — hardens the artifacts instructions after two observed failures: KARL narrated a deck it never emitted ("You'll find it open in the preview panel now"), and it leaked internal skill mechanics to the user ("the structure outlined in references/slides.md ... the KNAUF token set"). Four contradictions found by an adversarial pass over the full instruction surface and fixed here. **(1)** The `Artifacts:` paragraph said "follow them exactly" about `generateArtifactsPrompt`, which says "Prefer in-line content (don't use artifacts) when possible" (`prompts/artifacts/index.ts:219`) and "err on the side of not creating an artifact" (`:284`) — and lands AFTER the anchor in the same system message, so it won on both deference and recency. Now claims explicit precedence on WHEN to build, while still deferring on format/types/libraries. **(2)** `Sources:` ordered citation of "any file you retrieved", which includes skill files — the leak would have returned as a citation block. Narrowed to user-facing documents. **(3)** `Files:` triggered on the WORD "presentation" rather than on a named format, reopening the exact refusal this ticket fixes; now explicitly format-triggered. **(4)** The emission rule was a conditional (if you announce it, emit it), leaving "describe it and offer to build it" fully compliant — and that is literally what `karl-artifacts/SKILL.md` instructed as its last line. Now a positive imperative tied to a checkable precondition (loaded instructions → the reply must contain the artifact), with carve-outs for prior-turn artifacts and for when the user asked for the plan itself. Guarded by two new cases in `librechatYaml.knauf.spec.ts`. |
| `librechat.yaml` · `skill/karl-artifacts/references/*.md` · `skill/karl-brand/SKILL.md` | KK20-252 (fifth append for this ticket) — artifact-body emission discipline, after KARL shipped a deck whose file carried, after the component's closing brace, an HTML comment, prose, and mangled backtick fences; Sandpack threw `Unexpected token (97:0)` on the `<!-- -->`. The HTML comment was **displacement caused by the fourth append**: upstream orders the model to announce shadcn/ui (`packages/api/src/prompts/artifacts/index.ts:277`), that append banned saying it *in a reply*, and the model satisfied both by hiding it in a comment inside the file. Fixed by scoping the ban to audience rather than surface, and by three carve-outs an adversarial pass caught in the first attempt: `text/markdown` artifacts are prose end to end so a blanket no-prose rule would have broken them; the `KNAUF` block karl-brand orders transcribed verbatim is half comments, several load-bearing, so a blanket no-comment rule would have stripped them; and `Say nothing, anywhere` about libraries would have reached the `import` lines themselves, pushing the model to hand-roll primitives out of raw divs. Also adds the column-0 rule — the upstream format example is indented 6 spaces, and at 4+ spaces CommonMark takes the block as indented code so the directive never parses and the artifact lands in chat with no error — plus a ban on using a placeholder image for the Knauf mark and on the self-deprecating commentary upstream invites. The skill edits strip skill names and file paths from comment lines inside copy-verbatim blocks, which the new ban would otherwise have made unfollowable on its first real use. Guarded by three new cases in `librechatYaml.knauf.spec.ts` |
| `librechat.yaml` · `skill/karl-artifacts/SKILL.md` | KK20-252 (sixth append for this ticket) — artifact type routing. Asked for a presentation, KARL emitted a `text/markdown` artifact. It followed karl-artifacts' "Choose the right type" table correctly: that table never mentioned presentations or slide decks at all, and filed *report* under `text/markdown` — while the skill ships **tsx** templates for both (`references/slides.md`, `references/report.md`, both `export default` React components). The table contradicted the skill's own deliverables and predates every prompt change on this ticket. Table rewritten so decks, branded reports, briefings and one-pagers route to `application/vnd.react` and name their template, and the markdown row narrows to plain unstyled prose with no layout or branding; two rules of thumb added, including that "document deliverable" is not a routing rule. The same routing is stated in `&karl_prompt` as well, because a skill body only reaches the model once the skill is invoked and the type decision can be made before that. Guarded by a case in `librechatYaml.knauf.spec.ts` |
| `librechat.yaml` · `skill/karl-brand/**` · `skill/karl-artifacts/**` | KK20-252 (seventh append for this ticket) — flips branded artifacts from transcription to import, now that `/knauf/brand` is mounted in every sandbox (see the two Inline rows). KARL had emitted `import { useKnaufTheme, KNAUF } from '/knauf-brand-tokens'` — a module that did not exist — after earlier turns produced placeholder logos and trimmed token blocks; the model was routing around a design that asked it to retype 903 characters of coordinate data whose own reference says "any edit silently deforms the letterforms, and there is no error to tell you". Skills: `karl-brand/SKILL.md` now states the React path is an import and scopes transcription to media with no module system (HTML/SVG artifacts, documents, decks, email); its `KNAUF` block **stays** — it is where the values are authored, `references/non-artifact.md` still needs them, and removing it would strand four hexes that only exist there and in karl-artifacts' quick-card mirror, failing `deploymentSkills.knauf.spec.ts`'s hex guard. `karl-artifacts/SKILL.md` gains `/knauf/brand` in the import allowlist and loses the flat "no importing your own modules" prohibition that directly forbade it. The three layout templates open with the import instead of a paste preamble. `references/logo.md` keeps its raw-SVG block for the types that cannot import and drops the duplicate React copy — hence `toHaveLength(2)` → `toHaveLength(1)` in that spec. `librechat.yaml`: two clauses only. "import freely from everything available to you" → bounded to "the paths and libraries your artifact instructions list" (the unbounded grant is the plausible source of the invented path), and the wordmark sentence now names the import for React while keeping inline SVG for every other type. Nothing else in the anchor was touched. |
| `librechat.yaml` · `skill/karl-artifacts/SKILL.md` | NO-TICKET — a dev preview died with `Could not find module in path: '/components/ui'`. The shadcn files were mounted the whole time; the model wrote a barrel import that is not mounted, and `TabList`, which is not an export (shadcn's is `TabsList`). **Root cause is ours, not upstream's.** `karl-artifacts/SKILL.md` recommended `Card`, `Tabs`, `Table` and `Badge` by name while carrying an import example for only Card and Table (in the report and dashboard templates). The specs run `artifacts: 'default'`, which omits `generateShadcnPrompt` and therefore the whole `components.ts` manifest — so for `Tabs` and `Badge` the model had a recommendation, the bare pattern `/components/ui/<name>`, and nothing else. It extrapolated. `'shadcnui'` was tried and reverted: it fixes the symptom by shipping a 711-line catalogue of all 37 components at ~2.9k tokens/turn, to support the four the skill actually recommends. The fix is the skill carrying those four import lines with their real export names, plus a note that there is no barrel. Two guards, both negative-tested: `deploymentSkills.knauf.spec.ts` fails if any recommended component has no import example (naming `Tabs` and `Badge` when the block is removed) or if any skill points at the barrel; `librechatYaml.knauf.spec.ts` pins the mode, so flipping to `'shadcnui'` is a visible decision rather than a silent one. |
| `knauf/scripts/seed-agent-builder-role.js` | KK20-252 — `RUN_CODE: { USE: false }` on the AGENT_BUILDER (AI Champions) role, paired with `interface.runCode: false` in `librechat.yaml`. This role is seeded only by this script, never by `updateInterfacePermissions`, so it would otherwise have been the single remaining holder of RUN_CODE.USE after the yaml change — with a Run button backed by an undeployed sandbox. Set explicitly false rather than deleted because the script does `$set: { permissions }`, a full replace, and an absent permission type reads as deny without saying so. A human runs this via mongosh per environment after each release; until it is re-run, existing AGENT_BUILDER role documents keep the old grant. |

| `client/src/locales/{en,de}/translation.json` | Artifact repair copy — localizes the owner-only manual fallback action (NO-TICKET) |

| `librechat.yaml` | KK20-243 branding follow-up — the shared KARL prompt now makes Knauf branding a fixed parameter for Knauf/KARL visual deliverables, requires `karl-brand` before artifacts/documents/decks/spreadsheets/generated images, pins Knauf Screen + Knauf Screen Head and the exact clear-spaced SVG wordmark, and forbids image models from redrawing the logo. A named external brand remains an explicit carve-out. |
| `librechat.yaml` | Leaves prompt create/share and colleague discovery out of deployment-owned overrides so Admin Dashboard values persist; prompt use stays enabled and USER public sharing remains server-enforced off (NO-TICKET) |
| `client/src/locales/{en,de}/translation.json` | Localizes the Continue in KARL action, artifact-only context instructions, change-request cue, and failed-import message (NO-TICKET) |
| `client/src/locales/{en,de}/translation.json` | Keeps only the localized Continue in KARL action and clone-failure message; the former composer prompt is intentionally removed because continuation now opens a persisted recipient-owned artifact immediately (NO-TICKET) |
| `librechat.yaml` | Routes changes to existing artifacts through `edit_artifact`, requires only a short confirmation after success, and retains full source emission as the safe fallback (NO-TICKET) |

Strips leading YAML frontmatter before a SKILL.md becomes a `user` message. Fails **open** by design: an unterminated or absent block returns the input unchanged, because a skill body is load-bearing context and silently emptying it would be worse than leaving the frontmatter in. Deliberately strips at the injection boundary, not at load, so `DeploymentSkill.body` stays byte-faithful for the read-only skills API, for `read_file skills/<name>/SKILL.md`, and for `deploymentSkills.knauf.spec.ts` | `packages/api/src/knauf/skills/skillPrimeBody.spec.ts` (13 cases) | KK20-252 |
| `packages/api/src/knauf/skills/skillPrimeBody.spec.ts` | Subject only (`./skillPrimeBody`) plus `fs`/`path` to run the real `skill/` tree through it | Low — test-only. Pins that the frontmatter is removed and that **nothing else is**: a greedy regex would eat the body up to a mid-document `---` horizontal rule and nobody would see an error, the model would just behave slightly worse. Also asserts the strip is idempotent and that the shipped skills lose fewer than 600 chars each | Self | KK20-252 |
| `packages/api/src/knauf/config/librechatYaml.knauf.spec.ts` | `js-yaml`, `~/utils` (`getModelMaxTokens`), and the committed `librechat.yaml` | Low — test-only, but the first thing in the repo to validate the shipped `librechat.yaml` at all. `librechat.yaml` is baked into the image at build time and had no test, which is how KK20-243 shipped both a prompt that contradicted the artifacts prompt and a 32k context window nobody intended. Asserts: every spec whose model `getModelMaxTokens` cannot resolve pins `maxContextTokens`; no pin sits at or below the 32k fallback; the KARL chat prompt neither claims file generation is unsupported nor orders text-only replies while artifacts are enabled, yet still forbids PDF/XLSX; and summarization is stated rather than inferred from `undefined !== false`. Seam test for the two chip hides, deliberately *not* `Manual`: each case sets up the exact context that would make the chip visible upstream — capability enabled, toggle on, chip pinned, permission granted — so the empty render can only come from the seam. Verified by flipping both constants to `false`: all three cases fail. A third case pins that the chips stay hidden *without* the capability being switched off, guarding against a future "fix" that hides them by disabling artifacts/skills for the model too | Self | KK20-252 |
| `client/src/knauf/utils/artifactBody.ts` | `react`, `~/utils` (`extractContent`) | Low — one type-set membership test and one `children.find`, called from a single upstream site. Provably a no-op on a well-formed artifact, because both the upstream artifacts prompt and the `&karl_prompt` anchor define one as exactly one fence — it only diverges where the old behaviour was already producing an uncompilable file. `text/markdown` is deliberately excluded: a markdown artifact is a document, and prose between two code samples is real content. Fails open on a missing code block, which is the mid-stream state before the fence opens | `client/src/knauf/utils/artifactBody.knauf.spec.tsx` (8 cases; the two corrupted bodies reproduced verbatim from the Sandpack overlays) | KK20-252 |

Verified by disabling the filter: 4 of the 8 fail, the other 4 being the no-op/fall-open/markdown assertions that must hold either way | Self | KK20-252 |

Deliberately NOT added to `packages/data-provider`: CLAUDE.md bans Knauf-only additions there, and a const in that package is invisible to both the client build and client tests until `build:packages` runs. Untyped on purpose — Sandpack transpiles without type-checking, and these are the signatures already running in previews | `client/src/knauf/sandbox/brandModule.knauf.spec.ts` | KK20-252 |

Also tokenizes the module's `d` path against `knauf/brand/logos/KNAUFLogo2024sRGB.svg` (same yardstick as `deploymentSkills.knauf.spec.ts`, comparing geometry not bytes), and asserts the module is actually reachable at `sharedFiles['/knauf/brand.tsx']` — without that a perfect module could still never reach a preview, failing exactly like having no module at all. ## Static Assets

| File | Purpose | Merge Strategy |
|---|---|---|
| `knauf/brand/{logos,colours,guidelines,fonts}/**` | KK20-243 — the source-of-truth Knauf brand kit as delivered (17 files, ~26 MB: EPS and PNG logos for both the primary KNAUF and the Knauf Insulation sub-brand, the official colour system as HTML plus a print PDF, the visual-identity guidelines PDF, and a licensed font archive). **Reference only** — excluded from the Docker build context by `.dockerignore`, so nothing here is reachable at runtime, and a sandboxed artifact preview can load neither a binary asset nor a network request. Runtime assets are exported into `client/public/assets/` and tracked as their own rows above; the palette and wordmark that artifacts need are transcribed as text into `skill/karl-brand/`. The font archive is licensed and must not be embedded, self-hosted, or shipped to browsers without written web-embedding permission. Marked `-text -diff` in `.gitattributes`. | Knauf-only |
| `client/public/assets/{favicon-16x16,favicon-32x32,apple-touch-icon-180x180}.png` | KK20-159 — Knauf-branded PNG binaries replacing the upstream LibreChat-branded favicons + apple-touch-icon in place; `client/index.html` refs/filenames kept identical to upstream so the diff is binary-only. Future Tier 2 `Dockerfile.knauf` migration would move the sources under `knauf/branding/` and `COPY` them into the image. | Keep Knauf (ours) |
| `client/public/assets/knauf-logo.png` | KK20-159 — Knauf wordmark (2363×1196 PNG), net-new asset at an upstream path. Consumed by `Landing.tsx` (default-spec landing) and `KnaufTopBar.tsx` (top header) via a direct `<img src="/assets/knauf-logo.png">` — bypasses upstream `ConvoIcon`/`ConvoIconURL`, which only render iconURLs containing `http`. Future Tier 2 migration would move the source under `knauf/branding/`. | Knauf-only |
| `client/public/assets/knauf-social-preview.png` | Padded 1200×630 Open Graph/Twitter preview canvas with the Knauf wordmark centered, so chat clients and link unfurlers can crop previews without slicing the logo. | Knauf-only |
| `client/src/knauf/sandbox/brandFonts.ts` (embedded WOFF2 payloads) | KK20-243 follow-up — internal KARL artifact-preview use of the supplied Digital+Web KnaufScreen Regular/Bold and KnaufScreenHead Regular/Bold fonts. Embedded as data URLs so previews remain exact and offline; guarded byte-for-byte by SHA-256 tests. No CDN or external font service. | Knauf-only |
